# Awesome Herdr: Evaluated Ecosystem Guide

> **842 Herdr ecosystem repositories, categorized and assessed instead of merely listed.**

[Herdr](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr) is a persistent terminal runtime for coding agents. It owns workspaces, tabs, panes, process state, detach/reattach, agent status, and a local CLI/socket control surface. This guide maps the ecosystem that grows around those primitives: orchestration, MCP and socket clients, editor bridges, session tools, worktree automation, review interfaces, remote apps, hardware, packaging, and experiments.

```sh
curl -fsSL https://herdr.dev/install.sh | sh
herdr
```

**Official:** [Repository](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr) · [Documentation](https://herdr.dev/docs/) · [Plugin marketplace](https://herdr.dev/plugins/) · [Agent skill](https://herdr.dev/docs/agent-skill/) · [Socket API](https://herdr.dev/docs/socket-api/)

**Companion files:** [CSV](catalog-evaluations.csv) · [JSON](catalog-evaluations.json) · [Research notes](RESEARCH-NOTES.md) · [Audit manifest](AUDIT-MANIFEST.json) · [Generator](tools/build_readme.py)

> [!IMPORTANT]
> This guide is editorial. The official marketplace is an automatic discovery index, not a reviewed catalog. Herdr plugins are ordinary code that runs as your user and is not sandboxed or vetted by Herdr. Read `herdr-plugin.toml`, inspect build/runtime commands, check the license, and pin a revision before installing consequential code.

## How to use this guide

Start with the opinionated shortlist below, then search the exhaustive catalog by agent, language, platform, or workflow. Treat `Established` as “worth evaluating first,” not “safe by decree.” Treat `Unverified` as an invitation to inspect, not a condemnation. For any tool that sends input, runs commands, changes worktrees, merges code, exposes a web endpoint, or accepts remote replies, read the trust note before installation.

## Strong starting points

These are not universal winners. They are projects with comparatively strong evidence and a clear job. Choose the narrowest tool that solves your problem.

## Choose by architecture, not by logo

| Need | Prefer | Avoid by default |
|---|---|---|
| Give one agent Herdr literacy | The official release-matched skill | A full orchestration framework |
| Build your own automation | A typed socket client | Scraping terminal text when a structured API exists |
| Let an MCP client control Herdr | One MCP server with the narrowest useful tool profile | Running multiple overlapping MCP servers against the same session |
| See status without mutating work | Read-only viewer, dashboard, or notifier | Remote reply/control services |
| Coordinate parallel coding | Worktree isolation plus explicit reviewer gates | Several agents editing the same checkout |
| Review agent diffs | Read-only viewer or comment-to-agent review tool | Auto-merge before review conventions are stable |
| Work from a phone | Tailnet/SSH-first client or a carefully secured relay | Public unauthenticated tunnels |
| Tune keybindings | A small config or navigation plugin | Installing an entire personal dotfiles repository wholesale |

### Practical starter stacks

**Small local setup:** official skill + one session navigator + one read-only file/diff viewer.

**Neovim setup:** choose either a full editor-hosted workflow such as `herd.nvim` or a lighter navigation layer such as `herdr-splits.nvim`; add a session navigator separately.

**Parallel implementation setup:** worktree automation + a bounded orchestration tool + a review surface. Keep branch ownership explicit and make merge authority a human or a dedicated reviewer step.

**Remote setup:** choose one remote surface. Put it behind SSH or a private network, avoid broad command exposure, and document how access is revoked when a device or token is lost.

## Seven checks before installation

1. Open `herdr-plugin.toml` and list every build command, startup hook, event hook, action, pane, and supported platform.
2. Read the invoked scripts or binary release process. A clean manifest can still launch opaque code.
3. Decide whether the project is read-only, locally mutating, or remotely controllable. Do not treat those as equivalent trust decisions.
4. Look for network listeners, tunnels, Telegram/email bridges, SSH credentials, API tokens, transcript access, and exported environment variables.
5. Test branch deletion, worktree cleanup, session stop, auto-merge, and deploy behavior in a disposable repository.
6. Check the license, minimum Herdr version, runtime versions, platform support, release artifacts, checksums, and rollback instructions.
7. Pin a revision for consequential use and record how to revoke access, disable the plugin, remove state, and recover the previous workflow.

Herdr's interactive installer previews source and commands, but that preview is the beginning of review, not the finish line.

## Findings that materially change the old catalog

- The canonical Herdr repository is now `herdrdev/herdr`, and the official skill lives at `skills/herdr/SKILL.md`. `herdr --skill` prints the copy matched to the installed release.
- The source summary table undercounts its own detailed catalog by one entry. This edition includes all 842 detailed records, including `rohanthewiz/herdr-web`, which the summary omitted.
- The source watchlist has drifted. Several entries now have runnable implementations, tests, manifests, or release instructions, while others remain one-commit specifications. Source category and current readiness are therefore shown separately.
- The official marketplace and an editorial ecosystem guide serve different purposes. The marketplace discovers self-tagged plugin repositories automatically; this guide also covers skills, clients, editor integrations, configs, apps, packaging, hardware, and projects that are too early for recommendation.
- `cobanov/herdr-ntfysh` is archived. It remains a useful compact notification implementation to study, but should not be presented as an actively maintained default.
- `hungv47/herdr-agent-orchestration` explicitly tells readers to use Firstmate and describes itself as an experiment. It is marked superseded here.
- `thanhdat77/herdr-picker-plus` redirects to `thanhdat77/herdr-navigator`. Both source records are retained so readers can understand the rename instead of mistaking them for two maintained products.
- A large tail of entries has generic, repeated catalog prose rather than repository-specific evidence. Those projects are marked `Unverified` instead of receiving invented confidence.

## Scope and research method

This edition starts from the detailed records in the source catalog, not only its summary table. That matters because the source contains **842 unique repository records**, while its at-a-glance table contains 841 and omits one detailed watchlist entry. The missing record is included here.

The audit combines four evidence layers:

1. the source catalog's per-project descriptions and category placement;
2. current official Herdr documentation, release information, plugin model, and marketplace behavior;
3. current primary-source inspection for **64 projects**, prioritizing core infrastructure, high-impact control surfaces, ambiguous claims, redirects, archives, and every source-watchlist entry;
4. a consistent heuristic review for every remaining record, with weak evidence labelled weak instead of being polished into certainty.

**This is a documentation and repository-structure review. It is not a code-execution test, dependency audit, malware scan, penetration test, or guarantee of maintenance.** A high trust surface means a project can do more, not that it is malicious. A low trust surface does not mean a project is safe.

### Assessment vocabulary

| Dimension | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Readiness** | `Established` has strong current repository evidence; `Usable` has a concrete implementation and use case; `Reference` is primarily configuration or examples; `Experimental` is early; `Unverified` lacks enough evidence; `Watch` is design-stage; `Superseded` points elsewhere; `Archived` is no longer maintained. |
| **Evidence** | `High` means the current repository or explicit status was inspected; `Medium` means project-specific documentation is substantive; `Low` means the available description is thin, generic, or ambiguous. |
| **Setup** | `Light` is usually a skill, script, or configuration; `Moderate` adds a runtime, build, or plugin installation; `Heavy` adds services, remote access, mobile/desktop clients, cloud infrastructure, VMs, or hardware. |
| **Trust surface** | `Low` is mostly read-only or navigational; `Medium` can mutate local sessions or project state; `High` can execute broad automation, expose remote control, touch credentials, merge/deploy, or cross a network boundary. |

Categorical labels avoid fake precision. Stars, commit counts, and novelty are useful clues, but they are not quality scores. The deciding question is whether the repository exposes enough concrete evidence for its claimed job.

### Freshness policy

- **Audit date:** 2026-08-16
- **Stable Herdr reference:** v0.8.0
- The official marketplace is automatic and unreviewed. Its snapshot generated on 2026-08-15 contained **654 plugins from 643 repositories**, while this guide deliberately includes plugins, skills, clients, configurations, apps, packaging, and watchlist projects.
- Repository facts can change immediately after the audit. Open the project, inspect the manifest and executed commands, review the license, and pin a revision for consequential use.

## Editor integrations

Navigation, context transfer, REPLs, agent control, and editor-hosted Herdr workflows.

**60 projects.** Established: 2, Usable: 20, Reference: 1, Unverified: 37.

<details>
<summary><strong>Open the complete editor integrations catalog</strong></summary>

| Project | Best fit | Evaluation | Research note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **[devxplay/herdr.nvim](https://github.com/devxplay/herdr.nvim)**<br><sub>Lua, Rust · Neovim, Vim</sub> | **Neovim pane navigation**<br>Neovim ↔ Herdr pane navigation | **Usable**<br><sub>Medium evidence · Light setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | Unifies pane navigation between Neovim and Herdr: the same `Ctrl+h/j/k/l` that moves between Vim splits flows straight into the adjacent Herdr pane when you hit an edge, and back again. A small Rust helper talks to the socket for focus, splits, and layout, and it coexists with vim-tmux-navigator: Neovim detects whether it's inside Herdr or tmux and routes accordingly. |
| **[MomePP/herd.nvim](https://github.com/MomePP/herd.nvim)**<br><sub>Lua · Neovim</sub> | **Neovim-hosted agent workflow**<br>Neovim as host for Herdr agents | **Established**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Strong candidate</sub> | Makes Neovim the top-level UI for Herdr coding agents: spawn an agent into a fullscreen floating terminal, toggle it with one key, and push the current visual selection straight to the active agent without submitting. A broader editor-hosted workflow than a navigation shim. Review how it owns windows, terminal buffers, and agent actions before mixing it with similar plugins. |
| **[paulbkim-dev/vim-herdr-navigation](https://github.com/paulbkim-dev/vim-herdr-navigation)**<br><sub>Shell · Neovim, Vim</sub> | **Vim and Herdr split navigation**<br>Making Ctrl+h/j/k/l cross naturally between Vim or Neovim splits and adjacent Herdr panes. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Light setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A port of vim-tmux-navigator to Herdr: `Ctrl+h/j/k/l` flows between Vim/Neovim splits and adjacent Herdr panes, crossing the boundary at an edge in either direction. It runs as a real Herdr plugin action, checking the pane's foreground process via `herdr pane process-info` to decide whether to forward the key or move pane focus. |
| **[lmilojevicc/herdr-splits.nvim](https://github.com/lmilojevicc/herdr-splits.nvim)**<br><sub>Lua · Neovim</sub> | **Neovim and Herdr split navigation**<br>Smart split navigation and resizing | **Established**<br><sub>High evidence · Light setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Strong candidate</sub> | A port of smart-splits.nvim to Herdr that adds resizing to the navigation story: the same `Alt+h/j/k/l` that resizes Neovim splits delegates to Herdr when a window fills the terminal edge, and movement likewise crosses the boundary. Its value depends on consistent keybindings across Neovim, Herdr, and the outer terminal. Test edge navigation and resize behavior together. |
| **[luiarthur/herdr.vim](https://github.com/luiarthur/herdr.vim)**<br><sub>Vim Script · Neovim, Vim</sub> | **Vim-to-REPL bridge**<br>Spawning a Herdr-pane REPL and sending a Vim line, selection, or file to Julia, Python, or R. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Light setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A Vim and Neovim plugin that spawns a language-appropriate REPL in a Herdr pane and sends the current line, the whole file, or a visual selection to it with one key. Supports Vim 7.4 through 9 and Neovim 0.5+, with remappable defaults. The built-in language mapping is intentionally narrow, and its default Ctrl+h/j/k/l bindings overlap with common split-navigation conventions. |

## Session navigation, restore, and triage

Pickers, launchers, resume tools, hibernation, inboxes, and workspace switching.

**64 projects.** Established: 3, Usable: 52, Experimental: 1, Unverified: 7, Superseded: 1.

<details>
<summary><strong>Open the complete session navigation, restore, and triage catalog</strong></summary>

| Project | Best fit | Evaluation | Research note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **[ridho9/switchr](https://github.com/ridho9/switchr)**<br><sub>Go</sub> | **Session and pane picker**<br>TUI session picker with pane tree | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A full-screen session picker for Herdr: it lists every session next to its workspace / tab / pane tree, and you attach to the one you want with a keypress. Wire it up as your terminal's startup command and it greets you on each new window; it also spots an incompatible daemon and offers an in-place restart. |
| **[j0urneyk/herdrctx](https://github.com/j0urneyk/herdrctx)**<br><sub>Go · macOS, Linux</sub> | **Session control TUI**<br>TUI to attach, stop, manage sessions | **Established**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Strong candidate</sub> | A keyboard-driven TUI for the housekeeping side of sessions: attach, stop, delete, create, and search, without copying names out of `herdr session list`. It ships through a Homebrew tap with prebuilt macOS and Linux binaries and refuses to launch nested when you're already inside a Herdr pane. |
| **[nickmaglowsch/herdr-session-restore](https://github.com/nickmaglowsch/herdr-session-restore)**<br><sub>Python · Claude</sub> | **Session and layout restore**<br>Layout + Claude sessions across reboots | **Experimental**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Pilot only</sub> | Tags every Claude Code pane with a session ID, then snapshots your workspace / tab / cwd layout on a clean `herdr server stop` and replays it on the next cold boot: `claude --resume` and all: so your conversations come back where you left them. The repository had one commit at inspection time… |

## Worktrees and workspace lifecycle

Git worktrees, Jujutsu workspaces, project templates, setup hooks, and isolation.

Review surfaces, viewers, dashboards, keybindings, themes, copy tools, and quality-of-life plugins.

**285 projects.** Established: 3, Usable: 167, Reference: 1, Experimental: 1, Unverified: 112, Superseded: 1.

| **[justcyl/pi-herdr-tab-sync](https://github.com/justcyl/pi-herdr-tab-sync)**<br><sub>TypeScript · Pi</sub> | **Terminal utility**<br>Pi session names on your tabs | **Usable**<br><sub>Medium evidence · Moderate setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Candidate</sub> | Renames the active Herdr tab to match your Pi session name on resume or agent start, so you can tell what each tab is doing at a glance instead of decoding generic titles. It talks to the socket directly and only activates inside Herdr-managed panes, so it's safe to install globally and forget. |
| **[yigitkonur/native-shortcuts-herd](https://github.com/yigitkonur/native-shortcuts-herd)**<br><sub>TypeScript · macOS</sub> | **Terminal utility**<br>macOS-native keys in Ghostty + Herdr | **Usable**<br><sub>Medium evidence · Moderate setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Candidate</sub> | Patches Ghostty and Herdr together so `cmd+t`, `cmd+n`, `cmd+w`, `cmd+1..9`, and tab cycling behave the way they do in Chrome or Safari: no manual escape-sequence wiring. It routes the keys through a Ghostty sidecar file (never touching your main config), maps them to the matching Herdr actions, and keeps timestamped backups plus a clean uninstall path. macOS only. |

## Source watchlist and emerging projects

The source catalog placed these projects in a watchlist. Their current readiness is re-evaluated here: several now contain runnable code, while others remain design-only.

**16 projects.** Usable: 7, Experimental: 4, Watch: 5.

<details>
<summary><strong>Open the complete source watchlist and emerging projects catalog</strong></summary>

| Project | Best fit | Evaluation | Research note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **[rbb/herdr-cursor](https://github.com/rbb/herdr-cursor)**<br><sub>TypeScript · Cursor</sub> | **Cursor SDK integration specification**<br>Studying a proposed shim that would expose Cursor Agent SDK lifecycle state to Herdr through a socket-facing REPL. | **Watch**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Watchlist</sub> | A documentation-only proposal for exposing Cursor Agent SDK state to Herdr through a socket-facing REPL. The inspected repository had two commits and no runnable implementation; Herdr now also documents its own Cursor integration path, so use this as design reference rather than installable software. |
| **[shippy/raycast-herdr](https://github.com/shippy/raycast-herdr)**<br><sub>YAML</sub> | **Private Raycast control extension**<br>Launching Claude in configured folders, focusing Herdr workspaces, and running user-defined Herdr actions from Raycast on macOS. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Evaluate carefully</sub> | A working macOS Raycast extension for asking Claude in configured folders, focusing Herdr workspaces, and running user-defined Herdr actions. The repository includes extension source, tests, lint/build automation, publishing workflow, and an MIT license. It is designed for a private Raycast store or development install, and custom action JSON executes local commands. |
| **[SuperInstance/herdr-cocapn](https://github.com/SuperInstance/herdr-cocapn)**<br><sub>Rust</sub> | **Fleet-management Herdr fork**<br>Experimenting with tiered local and cloud agent fleets, overload deadbands, automatic escalation, and crossfade-style handoffs. | **Experimental**<br><sub>High evidence · Heavy setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Pilot only</sub> | An experimental Herdr 0.6.6 fork that layers tiered local/cloud fleet management, overload deadbands, escalation, and handoff behavior onto CoCapn. The repository includes source, examples, and CI, but the published Cargo manifest hardcodes `cocapn-core` to `/tmp/herdr-cocapn`, preventing a reproducible build without repair. Its autonomous control surface is broad. |
| **[rohanthewiz/herdr-web](https://github.com/rohanthewiz/herdr-web)**<br><sub>TypeScript · Web</sub> | **Browser-first terminal runtime**<br>Evaluating a self-contained Go browser terminal runtime with persistent PTYs, agent detection, worktrees, restore, WebSocket control, and optional remote access. | **Experimental**<br><sub>High evidence · Heavy setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Pilot only</sub> | A substantial, independent Go browser-terminal runtime inspired by Herdr, with persistent PTYs, agent detection, worktree support, restore, WebSocket control, authentication/TLS options, CI, and release automation. It is no longer a thin client for the canonical Herdr server. The project calls itself alpha, exposes a large remote-control surface, and no license was visible in the inspected root. |
| **[Matovidlo/herdr-pr-tracker](https://github.com/Matovidlo/herdr-pr-tracker)**<br><sub>JavaScript · Claude</sub> | **Agent pull-request operations board**<br>Tracking CI, mergeability, review state, comments, and GitHub pull requests associated with active Claude Code sessions. | **Experimental**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Pilot only</sub> | A developed pull-request operations board for Claude Code sessions, covering CI, mergeability, review state, comments, worktrees, rebase, push, merge, and agent-assisted review actions. The repository includes a manifest and MIT license, but the public install example still contains `&lt;you&gt;` and the manifest namespace is `martinv.pr-tracker`, so public distribution is not fully normalized. |
| **[makyinmars/muster](https://github.com/makyinmars/muster)**<br><sub>Swift · macOS</sub> | **Native macOS command-center specification**<br>Following a proposed SwiftUI/AppKit command center built around Herdr sessions, Ghostty rendering, a local MCP bridge, and a later iPhone companion. | **Watch**<br><sub>High evidence · Heavy setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Watchlist</sub> | A detailed SwiftUI/AppKit product and architecture specification for a native macOS command center around Herdr sessions, Ghostty rendering, a local MCP bridge, and a later iPhone companion. At inspection time it contained design documents but no Swift source or build, and explicitly described itself as a planning scaffold. |
| **[eliasstravik/herdr-call](https://github.com/eliasstravik/herdr-call)**<br><sub>TypeScript</sub> | **Tailnet voice-control bridge**<br>Checking agent status and steering Herdr sessions by voice from a private tailnet device while away from the keyboard. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Heavy setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Evaluate carefully</sub> | A TypeScript voice-control bridge with source, tests, CI, a plugin manifest, license, and an explicit security model. It uses Tailscale and ElevenLabs to report agent state and translate spoken requests into guarded Herdr navigation and input actions. The third-party voice service, stored API key, transcript-derived context, and command execution still require a serious threat-model review. |
| **[hmu332233/herdr-plugins-labs](https://github.com/hmu332233/herdr-plugins-labs)**<br><sub>Rust</sub> | **Plugin incubator monorepo**<br>Trying early quick-agent, workspace tab-count, and workspace-stat plugins before they graduate into independent repositories. | **Experimental**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Pilot only</sub> | An explicitly experimental Rust incubator for candidate Herdr plugins, including quick-agent launching, workspace tab counts, and workspace statistics. The monorepo has concrete plugin code and an MIT license, but each subproject should be evaluated independently and interfaces may change before graduation. |
| **[malone-c/herdr-pane-balancer](https://github.com/malone-c/herdr-pane-balancer)**<br><sub>Shell</sub> | **Automatic pane-layout balancer**<br>Keeping columns and stacked panes evenly proportioned whenever panes open or close in a Herdr tab. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A tested Python-backed plugin that automatically rebalances column and stacked-pane proportions when panes open or close. It has a manifest and concrete compatibility requirements: Herdr 0.7.5+, `uv`, and Python 3.11+. Automatic mode also affects plugin sidebars unless disabled. |
| **[meerzulee/herdr-float](https://github.com/meerzulee/herdr-float)**<br><sub>Shell</sub> | **Persistent floating shell**<br>Opening one native popup shell per Herdr workspace without changing the tiled pane layout. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A runnable shell plugin that opens one persistent, workspace-scoped floating shell through a native Herdr popup while keeping the tiled pane layout intact. It requires Herdr 0.7.4+, tmux, jq, and Bash. Shells live in a private tmux server and should be stopped before uninstalling; no license was visible in the inspected root. |
| **[shoaibkhanz/herdr-nav-plus](https://github.com/shoaibkhanz/herdr-nav-plus)**<br><sub>Shell · Vim</sub> | **Vim-aware pane and workspace navigation**<br>Using Ctrl+h/j/k/l to move across Vim splits, Herdr panes, and vertically adjacent workspaces with wraparound. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Light setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A tested, MIT-licensed navigation plugin that makes Ctrl+h/j/k/l move across Vim splits, Herdr panes, and vertically adjacent workspaces with wraparound. The direct control chords can collide with terminal applications or other navigation plugins, and cross-workspace traversal applies only to the vertical j/k axis. |
| **[ugurtarlig/herdr-pane-picker](https://github.com/ugurtarlig/herdr-pane-picker)**<br><sub>Rust</sub> | **On-pane character-hint picker**<br>Focusing a visible pane by typing a one-character overlay hint instead of walking the pane grid directionally. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Low trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A released, tested Rust plugin that overlays one-character hints on visible panes for direct keyboard focus. It includes CI, a manifest, changelog, and MIT license. The current path is macOS-only and requires Herdr 0.7.4+, Python 3.9+, a Kitty-graphics-compatible terminal, and Herdr experimental graphics; the faster popup-free path also depends on WezTerm. |
| **[wraithyy/herdr-openr](https://github.com/wraithyy/herdr-openr)**<br><sub>TypeScript · Claude</sub> | **Transcript and viewport jump picker**<br>Fuzzy-opening files and URLs mentioned in a pane viewport or Claude Code session transcript at the relevant line. | **Usable**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · Medium trust</sub><br><sub>Strong specialist</sub> | A working TypeScript plugin that scans a pane viewport and Claude Code JSONL transcripts, extracts file paths and URLs, presents them in a fuzzy picker, and dispatches configurable editor, browser, reveal, or clipboard actions. It includes a manifest and MIT license. Startup can add keybindings, and paths containing spaces are a documented limitation. |
| **[GoCodeAlone/mission-control-provider-herdr](https://github.com/GoCodeAlone/mission-control-provider-herdr)**<br><sub>Shell</sub> | **Mission Control runtime-provider scaffold**<br>Tracking the idea of using Herdr as an external session runtime behind the Mission Control project. | **Watch**<br><sub>High evidence · Moderate setup · High trust</sub><br><sub>Watchlist</sub> | A documentation-only proposal for using Herdr as an external session runtime behind Mission Control. The inspected repository had one commit containing a README, gitignore, and Apache-2.0 license, with no provider implementation, installation path, or protocol contract. |

No executable code, command contract, installation instructions, tests, or license were visible at inspection time. |

The heading still says `herdvim`, and no Lua implementation, installation instructions, tests, or license were visible at inspection time. |

</details>

## Inclusion and maintenance policy

An ecosystem guide becomes a junk drawer unless it has a bar. New entries should satisfy all of the following:

1. **Direct Herdr relevance:** Herdr is part of the working implementation, not a keyword in a roadmap or generated description.
2. **Concrete evidence:** the repository explains what it does, how to install or run it, and what platforms or dependencies it requires.
3. **Honest maturity:** prototypes and design documents are welcome only in the watchlist and must say what is not implemented.
4. **Traceable execution:** plugins should expose `herdr-plugin.toml`; scripts and skills should make their invoked commands inspectable.
5. **License clarity:** a license is strongly preferred and required before a project can be promoted into a recommended starter stack.
6. **No duplicate aliases:** renamed repositories should redirect to one canonical entry, with the old name retained only as a migration note.

A contribution should add or update: project link, one specific use-case sentence, installation shape, supported platform or agent, trust-surface note, maturity evidence, and one meaningful limitation. “Makes Herdr better” is not evidence. A tiny repository can still earn a strong assessment when its scope, implementation, and caveats are crisp.

### Suggested review cadence

- On every stable Herdr release: verify the canonical skill, API changes, minimum versions, and official links.
- Weekly or automatically: detect redirects, archives, deleted repositories, manifest changes, and marketplace additions.
- Monthly: revisit `Experimental`, `Unverified`, and `Watch` projects; promote only when the repository itself supplies stronger evidence.
- Never rank by stars alone. Prefer compatibility, testability, maintenance signals, installation transparency, and a bounded trust surface.
